## 14. Required evidence checklist

This list is the single source for gate evidence (lesson G1). It is contiguous, E1–E25. The final-regression receipt (E25) must list every ID with its producing commit, artifact paths and SHA-256 before acceptance starts.

| ID | Evidence item | Producer | Revision(s) | Gates |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| E1 | Sol oracle files and runner frozen with a SHA-256 receipt; the lockfile gate recorded; the archive byte count and streaming or buffer method; the F-B002 spy self-check; the in-domain seed table; the §12 consistency matrix with case references | Sol | `f9eb4b1` | 1–4 |

**Rules.**
- E1–E5 must be committed before Terra starts.
- E25 is produced last and enumerates every other item.
- A later item cannot substitute for a missing earlier one.
- Acceptance re-derives at least one hash per item and BLOCKS on any absent ID.
- **Judging copies.** The frozen More `boundaries`, Features `downstream` (cases 012 and 014) and Appearance `continuity-export` (cases 006 and 007) failures are judged by their copies: C-FB002, C-RD1 and OE. C-FD1 runs beside them with its predicted outcome and judges nothing. A judging copy that fails is a regression.
- **K-1 and transport.** Every native runner written for this caller uses pipe transport, sends no `nativeVirtualKeyCode` and audits keys (K-1). Reused frozen runners that send none run unchanged, with their frozen transport (AppRail acceptance §6 item 10).
- **Drags.** Native drags use trusted CDP input only (§12 rule 14).
- **Product-delta preconditions.** A reused frozen runner may refuse at a precondition bound to an earlier caller's product delta. The verifier then commits that refusal log, and runs a copy that replaces only that precondition with a stricter one naming this caller's §11 delta. This follows the Appearance and AppRail E24/E25 precedents, and the deviation is disclosed for acceptance. Every other precondition and assertion stays unchanged.
- **Capacity copies (pre-registered; AppRail acceptance §6 item 10).**
  - **When.** A reused frozen runner aborts with `ENOBUFS` (or any buffer-capacity error) while reading `git archive`, before any test runs.
  - **What the verifier does.** It commits the refusal transcript. It then runs a copy that differs from the frozen runner only in the archive buffer (sized from the measured archive, at least twice its size, or replaced by streaming) and, if the copy lives in a deeper directory, in its `root` depth, plus a header comment.
  - **Requirements.** The copy's diff against the frozen runner is committed. Staged test and fixture files must be byte-identical to the frozen ones. Counts must equal the accepted receipts and the `f9eb4b1` control.
  - **Pre-registered for:** the four Dashboard Header runners (E17), whose 100 MiB buffer is below the `f9eb4b1` archive.
  - **Applies on refusal to:** the three 200 MiB F1 runners `verify-f1.mjs`, `verify-f1-callers.mjs` and `verify-f1-race.mjs` (E15). Their buffer, 209,715,200 bytes, exceeds the 173,905,920-byte archive at the r2 docs head, but later evidence may outgrow it.
  - **Status.** The copy procedure is not a contract revision and needs no separate ruling batch; acceptance reviews each copy.
- **Not rerun.** The Features native host and downstream suites (Features E12/E13) and the AppRail native E9–E14 are not rerun. Their subjects (Features rail departures and drags, and AppRail's own surfaces) are outside the Dashboard packages, and the empty shell, `App.tsx` and tokens diff (E19) plus the `.app-rail`/`.topbar` invariance (E12) bound them. If either bound fails, they must be rerun.

## 3. Independent assessment of real technical findings and viability

### Shared bytes and Outer

Original complete byte review6c supports only its actual four-root capture. Its seven dependency misses came from nested pnpm lookup, not absent packages. ### BRD-12

The full1de3 review's two findings are substantive proposed-protocol problems. Source3a7 sections6–7 put RecoveryTarget.raw inside LossPlan.targets and the complete LossPlan in durable boardLoss. P0 accountDataLifecycle excludes the deleted tombstone from erasure and spreads the current receipt into local-data-cleared/complete. Thus the proposed final receipt preserves content supposedly deleted. Author4 must define phase-specific raw versus metadata proof, interrupted/redaction/readback handling, exact authorization/export/target identity and legacy confirmed-cleanup semantics. Removing audit accountability or weakening deletion is not an acceptable shortcut.

The same proposal's fresh-generation restore changes a global account marker. Direct P0 accountMigration enumerates all account-owned generation records, stages/verifies secrets and commits the marker last; rollback restores previousRaw or removes the marker when null. AccountDataGate forwards secrets to migrateAccount but not rollbackAccount; AccountStorageGate supplies aiSecretMigrationParticipant. That participant decrypts prior generation data and re-encrypts/verifies new account+generation ciphertext. Therefore a Board-only fresh candidate or blind ciphertext copy cannot define whole-account Undo this import. Exact non-Board source selection, secret stage/verify, journal/collision/readback/post-await/marker-last and null-previousRaw behavior must be frozen together, without silently granting protected secret/gate/test/docs paths.

The remaining complete T1–T4, B01–B12/R01–R12/W01–W21 contract remains: account L then sorted dataset K; all alternate writers/participants; task-link three-phase ownership; whole inverse conflict refusal; terminal retirement and foreign/import history non-executability; opaque raw recovery/unknown metadata; route/account/sign-out/reset/deletion/fallback host ordering and full affected evidence. Original80 future candidate paths are not a current grant and may be insufficient for a correct full-account protocol. Failed author3's thirteen extra path ideas are not artifacts. E2 permits one complete documentary protocol proposal plus existing full review3; it cannot guarantee correctness, authorize scope growth or prove universal old/cached-client quiescence. BLOCKED remains lawful if those grounds cannot be supplied.

### CD-03

Full0d4 review R3-01 is independently supported by P0 accountScope.ts: accountPrefix and generationMarkerKey take accountId:string and call trim; the source contract passes AccountScope. The exact adapter must preserve captured object identity while binding actual accountId/demo/null/locked argument semantics. Protected helpers are not thereby editable.

R3-02 is a separate observable-protocol defect. P0 sameTabBus catches physicalKey denial and returns a no-op closure, while success also returns a closure. A tombstone failure confined to subscription can coexist with successful surrounding reads and a later missed real public publication. A successful reread does not certify listener registration. E3 correctly requires a finite, source-grounded binding/rebind protocol including this exact fault and later public event; if unavailable within the proposed adapter and unchanged bus, author3 must report scope-impact BLOCKED. No protected-bus/helper grant, unconditional polling, missing fault control or silent new key/API is implied.

The whole14 original rows and C/K/Q relation remain mandatory: selected no-reminder/date-difference policy; source-read classifications/whole-array validation including invisible rows; exact raw snapshot/owner/marker identity; retained draft/conflict on refresh; current-date/custom/preset/history behavior; actual export default-action and disk proof; real public managed host/source closure; full23 product and18 machinery candidate sets, still conditional. E3 creates review capacity, not actual source qualification or a successful correction.

### DASH-06

Complete6fd review supports A16's restricted source-read design conditionally and identifies one concrete self-contained document defect: the10159-byte canonical prefix omits Rules while E15/E17 refer to Rules. This task consumes whole impact review1/3 and exactly one concluding documentary static1/1. Runtime/tests/typecheck/build/lint/server/browser/native/qualification/probes/vendor/children0. The current checker's actual PID, terminal numeric exit, captured stdout/stderr and owned Git-child exit/EOF/wait evidence, tool session/chunk availability, output digests, commit and clean status will be reported externally after its actual completion. They are not predicted inside this pre-write document. A missing tool PID/session/chunk remains UNKNOWN, never0. Read-only inspection commands are not extra concluding checks. Receipt/source hashes do not qualify recursive bundles.

On success exactly review.md and inputs.sha256 are ADDed in this directory, exact-path staged, with one hook-disabled real-newline Why/What/Scope/Risk/Docs/Tests commit; no amendment or push.   - the Smart Lists, Collaborate and Pomodoro host suites through the accepted buffer copies in `../web-apprail-order-recovery-final/host-suites/`, with the per-mode counts of that receipt's §6;
  - settings-shell 11 files / 54; settings-rest 44 files / 314.

**Rules.**
- E1–E5 must be committed before Terra starts.
- E25 is produced last and enumerates every other item.
- A later item cannot substitute for a missing earlier one.
- Acceptance re-derives at least one hash per item and BLOCKS on any absent ID.
- **Judging copies.** The frozen More `boundaries`, Features `downstream` (cases 012 and 014) and Appearance `continuity-export` (cases 006 and 007) failures are judged by their copies: C-FB002, C-RD1 and OE. C-FD1 runs beside them with its predicted outcome and judges nothing. A judging copy that fails is a regression.
- **K-1 and transport.** Every native runner written for this caller uses pipe transport, sends no `nativeVirtualKeyCode` and audits keys (K-1). Reused frozen runners that send none run unchanged, with their frozen transport (AppRail acceptance §6 item 10).
- **Drags.** Native drags use trusted CDP input only (§12 rule 14).
- **Product-delta preconditions.** A reused frozen runner may refuse at a precondition bound to an earlier caller's product delta. The verifier then commits that refusal log, and runs a copy that replaces only that precondition with a stricter one naming this caller's §11 delta. This follows the Appearance and AppRail E24/E25 precedents, and the deviation is disclosed for acceptance. Every other precondition and assertion stays unchanged.
- **Capacity copies (pre-registered; AppRail acceptance §6 item 10).**
  - **When.** A reused frozen runner aborts with `ENOBUFS` (or any buffer-capacity error) while reading `git archive`, before any test runs.
  - **What the verifier does.** It commits the refusal transcript. It then runs a copy that differs from the frozen runner only in the archive buffer (sized from the measured archive, at least twice its size, or replaced by streaming) and, if the copy lives in a deeper directory, in its `root` depth, plus a header comment.
  - **Requirements.** The copy's diff against the frozen runner is committed. Staged test and fixture files must be byte-identical to the frozen ones. Counts must equal the accepted receipts and the `f9eb4b1` control.
  - **Pre-registered for:** the four Dashboard Header runners (E17), whose 100 MiB buffer is below the `f9eb4b1` archive.
  - **Applies on refusal to:** the three 200 MiB F1 runners `verify-f1.mjs`, `verify-f1-callers.mjs` and `verify-f1-race.mjs` (E15). Their buffer, 209,715,200 bytes, exceeds the 173,905,920-byte archive at the r2 docs head, but later evidence may outgrow it.
  - **Status.** The copy procedure is not a contract revision and needs no separate ruling batch; acceptance reviews each copy.
- **Not rerun.** The Features native host and downstream suites (Features E12/E13) and the AppRail native E9–E14 are not rerun. Their subjects (Features rail departures and drags, and AppRail's own surfaces) are outside the Dashboard packages, and the empty shell, `App.tsx` and tokens diff (E19) plus the `.app-rail`/`.topbar` invariance (E12) bound them. If either bound fails, they must be rerun.

  - Sol `bytes` 65, `fields` 89, `reset` 34, `queues` 56, `host` 33, `retry-all` 48 and `original` 187;
  - `continuity-export`: frozen 24/26 (006, 007), recorded, **and** the OE copy `../web-appearance-recovery-oracle-erratum/verify-erratum.mjs … corrected` 26/26, judging;

  - `downstream` three ways: frozen 13/15 and C-FD1 14/15, both recorded, and C-RD1 15/15, judging;
  - host 40; package 7 files / 45; reader tests 5 files / 17.

  - Sol `fields` 22, `reset` 20, `queues` 14 and `owner-export` 13; `original` 15; host 11;
  - `boundaries`: frozen, recorded as it falls, **and** the C-FB002 copy `../web-more-recovery-fb002/verify-fb002.mjs … corrected full` 10/10, judging.

  - Sticky: Sol 109, original 10, host 28;
  - Notifications: Sol 41, Astra boundaries 24, Astra host 15, parent host 12;
  - Date & Time 7;
  - the Smart Lists, Collaborate and Pomodoro host suites through the accepted buffer copies in `../web-apprail-order-recovery-final/host-suites/`, with the per-mode counts of that receipt's §6;

**Rules.**
- E1–E5 must be committed before Terra starts.
- E25 is produced last and enumerates every other item.
- A later item cannot substitute for a missing earlier one.
- Acceptance re-derives at least one hash per item and BLOCKS on any absent ID.
- **Judging copies.** The frozen More `boundaries`, Features `downstream` (cases 012 and 014) and Appearance `continuity-export` (cases 006 and 007) failures are judged by their copies: C-FB002, C-RD1 and OE. C-FD1 runs beside them with its predicted outcome and judges nothing. A judging copy that fails is a regression.
- **K-1 and transport.** Every native runner written for this caller uses pipe transport, sends no `nativeVirtualKeyCode` and audits keys (K-1). Reused frozen runners that send none run unchanged, with their frozen transport (AppRail acceptance §6 item 10).
- **Drags.** Native drags use trusted CDP input only (§12 rule 14).
- **Product-delta preconditions.** A reused frozen runner may refuse at a precondition bound to an earlier caller's product delta. The verifier then commits that refusal log, and runs a copy that replaces only that precondition with a stricter one naming this caller's §11 delta. This follows the Appearance and AppRail E24/E25 precedents, and the deviation is disclosed for acceptance. Every other precondition and assertion stays unchanged.
- **Capacity copies (pre-registered; AppRail acceptance §6 item 10).**
  - **When.** A reused frozen runner aborts with `ENOBUFS` (or any buffer-capacity error) while reading `git archive`, before any test runs.
  - **What the verifier does.** It commits the refusal transcript. It then runs a copy that differs from the frozen runner only in the archive buffer (sized from the measured archive, at least twice its size, or replaced by streaming) and, if the copy lives in a deeper directory, in its `root` depth, plus a header comment.
  - **Requirements.** The copy's diff against the frozen runner is committed. Staged test and fixture files must be byte-identical to the frozen ones. Counts must equal the accepted receipts and the `f9eb4b1` control.
  - **Pre-registered for:** the four Dashboard Header runners (E17), whose 100 MiB buffer is below the `f9eb4b1` archive.
  - **Applies on refusal to:** the three 200 MiB F1 runners `verify-f1.mjs`, `verify-f1-callers.mjs` and `verify-f1-race.mjs` (E15). Their buffer, 209,715,200 bytes, exceeds the 173,905,920-byte archive at the r2 docs head, but later evidence may outgrow it.
  - **Status.** The copy procedure is not a contract revision and needs no separate ruling batch; acceptance reviews each copy.
- **Not rerun.** The Features native host and downstream suites (Features E12/E13) and the AppRail native E9–E14 are not rerun. Their subjects (Features rail departures and drags, and AppRail's own surfaces) are outside the Dashboard packages, and the empty shell, `App.tsx` and tokens diff (E19) plus the `.app-rail`/`.topbar` invariance (E12) bound them. If either bound fails, they must be rerun.

### 5.1 T39 consumed priority entry, protected-field exception and future literal oracle

The priority table at PLUGIN_MAP.md:13–17 has Tier/Surface/Status/Packages headers. Current parsePluginMap accepts every table with a Status header, uses Tier as name and Packages as path, and normalizes Status while retaining raw_status. T39 changes both Status and Packages. The source-level inference is therefore an intentional consumed data delta, not a priority-only edit that leaves entries or count payloads identical. Surface text also changes in the authored row but is not emitted by this parser. The name remains the literal **P2** including its Markdown stars; splitMarkdownRow only trims cells.

Future before entry literal (not executed here):

```json
{
  "name": "**P2**",
  "path": "`plugin-organizer`, `plugin-clipboard`, `plugin-widgets`, `plugin-meditation`, `plugin-pet`, plus sync-v1 crypto stack (W0/W1/W2/W3) and G2 data-security foundation (stay paused till G1 SHIPPED per ADR-0010 §D2)",
  "status": "PAUSED_UNTIL_P1_BETA",
  "raw_status": "Paused until P1 beta"
}
```

Future fixed entry literal (not executed here):

```json
{
  "name": "**P2**",
  "path": "`plugin-clipboard`, `plugin-widgets`, `plugin-pet`, planned-only `plugin-meditation`, plus sync-v1 crypto stack and G2 remain paused. G1 plugin-platform runtime is the active gate, physically executed on the App lane. Organizer is a Stable/shipped flagship outside the P2/P3 queue (Web ADR-0015 Accepted); dev ADR-0011 reconciliation remains operator-gated.",
  "status": "PAUSED_UNTIL_G1_SHIPPED",
  "raw_status": "Paused until G1 SHIPPED"
}
```

Here priorityStatus means this table's authored Status cell, not a new schema field. The exact protected-field exceptions are that one priority row's Surface, Packages/path, raw_status and normalized status, followed by their deterministic derived effects. All other plugin_map.entries fields/rows and real plugin/roadmap source statuses are byte/identity-protected. No table header, delimiter grammar, row insertion/deletion/reordering, regex membership, helper/API/status vocabulary or generated schema change is proposed.

For a future source-isolated before/fixed pair whose only PLUGIN_MAP change is T39, define O=PAUSED_UNTIL_P1_BETA and N=PAUSED_UNTIL_G1_SHIPPED. Both global plugin_map.status_counts and product_lines[key=plugin].status_counts must move exactly one contribution from O to N: fixed[O]=before[O]-1, fixed[N]=before[N]+1, interpreting a missing key as zero only for this comparison; a zero-count key must be absent because countByStatus constructs keys from actual entries. Every other bucket is identical; totals and ordered entry identities are unchanged. Do not assert either absolute bucket total without lawful actual before capture. overview_modules must carry exactly the same resulting module data. The fixed P2 path still matches the unchanged plugin_path_regex via clipboard/widgets/pet; deleting Organizer from the priority Packages description does not remove the real Organizer entry or move it to another module.

status_summary keeps its total prefix and formatCounts ordering rule, but its unknown-token suffix changes by the exact O/N bucket delta. Its literal keys are PAUSED_UNTIL_P1_BETA:<before count> and PAUSED_UNTIL_G1_SHIPPED:<fixed count>, with zero buckets omitted. Neither scalar is the PAUSED enum. progressFromCounts has zero numerator weight for both, the denominator is unchanged, and the same fallback/min/max/rounding applies, so T39 alone leaves progress numerically equal. pendingSummary and badgeForCounts branches are not newly triggered by either token; only the explicitly proposed authored fallback/running/phase strings may separately change. No current absolute progress/count result is claimed.

utils.js statusChips does not alias these unknown keys to PAUSED. It replaces underscores with spaces and uses b-gray: before chip text PAUSED UNTIL P1 BETA <count>, fixed chip text PAUSED UNTIL G1 SHIPPED <count>, ordered by the unchanged unknown-key sort. Product Structure's real-count chips and Overview drawer chips must show this exact payload transition (HTML escaping retained), with removal/retention of an old chip governed by the actual remaining bucket count. ## 9. Documentary check, process receipts, costs and holds

All complete inputs, this entire contract and the entire inputs.sha256 buffer are assembled in memory before any filesystem write, including drafts or index files. One own documentary static invocation re-reads each immutable Git/external source, checks its complete SHA-256 against captured and declared bindings, validates exact manifest grammar, actual parent/card/clean/absent output scope, full UTF-8/LF output buffers, every original and newly added replacement and H01, precise JSON-pointer applicability and unchanged outside-pointer data. Whole section/record coverage is derived from actual original documents and structures, not guessed counts, minimum sizes, headlines or brittle prose witnesses. It neither imports/runs the product parser nor reuses a business verifier. Human documentary reasoning remains distinct from this integrity result.

Any actual concluding FAIL/nonzero freezes the unit immediately: no retry, post-failure draft/materialization, commit or amend. Static wall cap120sec, owned child drain cap30sec, task wall cap30min. The checker PID, numeric exit, stdout/stderr, owned Git-child close/EOF and tool session/chunk are reported in the final immutable handoff; unexposed values remain UNKNOWN, never zero. Final buffer digests are external to these buffers to avoid self-reference. No Python newer write_text newline argument is used; source/output UTF-8 and actual newlines are explicit.

    "source_sha": "a9b22ebbec423028960f3c7332a6526f9c04ff45",
    "source_sha256": "5d7f7e7cf51961897dea8522ba73538f1d41be2db3a3a588321c35600cdb9c0c"

  {
    "path": "docs/workflow/project/dashboard-state.json",

### GOV06-R2-01 [P1] Account for T39's actual parser and count payload delta

Contract lines 144 and 177 claim the priority-only edit leaves plugin entries untouched; lines 95–112 and 166–177 describe preserved derived counts/projection and regressions. T39 at contract lines 549–555 changes PLUGIN_MAP.md:17, including the **Status** cell from `Paused until P1 beta` to `Paused until G1 SHIPPED`, as well as the Packages cell.

That priority table is consumed. generate-state.mjs:1507–1524 accepts every table with a Status/状态 header and maps Tier to name, Packages to path, and Status to status/raw_status. The table at PLUGIN_MAP.md:13–17 satisfies those exact conditions. normalizeStatus at :1478–1488 consequently changes this P2 entry's scalar status from `PAUSED_UNTIL_P1_BETA` to `PAUSED_UNTIL_G1_SHIPPED`. Its raw_status and path also change. The authored plugin regex at dashboard-state.json: product_lines[key=plugin].tracking.plugin_path_regex matches both the old and proposed Packages strings through clipboard/widgets/pet; buildProductLines at :1643–1648 therefore carries the renamed status bucket into the plugin module. Global plugin_map.status_counts changes too; status_summary includes the renamed key via :1588–1603. Product Structure renders status_counts at product-flow.js:263, and Overview renders them at overview.js:430.

This is a deterministic source-level impact inference, not an executed parser result or a claimed current count. T39 does not change the number of this table's rows, the regex membership of this P2 row, or either status token's zero contribution to progressFromCounts at :1606–1617. It still changes consumed status/count **payloads**. Preserving the algorithm is different from preserving those payloads.

Required correction: make this precise source-driven entry/bucket/display delta explicit in the contract, protected-field exceptions and future before/fixed/integrated consumer oracle, or redesign the documentary proposal so its preservation claims are actually true. Keep real plugin/roadmap entries, statuses, raw source and parser implementation protected. Do not expand to editing parsePluginMap/shared helpers to hide the impact. This matters to the whole consumer contract even though it needs no runtime/code fix in this review.

### GOV06-R2-02 [P1] Complete the G1/Organizer rule across App-to-Plugin mirrors

T15 at contract lines 332–339 correctly changes PRODUCT_MODULE_MAP.md:206 to distinguish active G1 runtime, delivered Organizer and unfinished packages awaiting G1. Appendix B does not carry the corresponding correction into dashboard-state.json:1245, `/product_lines/1/transitions/0/note`, which still ends “注意 P2 插件线在 G1 SHIPPED 前仍为 paused,需操作者解冻。”

The same broader freeze survives in both PRODUCT_MODULE_MAP.md:217 and dashboard-state.json:1281, `/product_lines/1/impacts/3/action`. Those entries tell an App host/SDK/window-command caller how to update the plugin platform and affected plugins; their blanket P2-plugin-line warning fails to distinguish the active runtime from unfinished package work and the shipped Organizer exception.

This is visible operational copy. The generator spreads authored product objects at :1679–1698; product-flow.js:194–210 and :274–275 renders both transition notes and impact actions. The proposed map row and generated dashboard detail would therefore disagree after exactly the registered A/B changes. The generic summary corrections elsewhere do not repair the literal copied/rendered instructions.

Review every corresponding App-to-Plugin occurrence together. This is factual mirror parity under the already accepted authorities; no new freeze/unfreeze decision is needed.
